## Synthetic Data Generation & AI Safety Testing {#synthetic-data-generation-ai-safety-testing}

### DeepEvals TaskCompletion Metric to evaluate the AI Agent - Blackbox Testing {#deepevals-taskcompletion-metric-to-evaluate-the-ai}

```
1
Welcome back.
2
So in this lecture, let's get started with the first metric of task completion metric. OK.
3
So this is part of deep evals library.
4
Now if you go to their official website, deep eval dot com.
5
So go to the docs and here where you can get complete information about this library and
6
the metrics they provide.
7
So there are different systems we deal with.
8
We deal with AI agents, chatbots, rag applications, agentic, MCP.
9
So these are all part of AI family. OK.
10

11

12
Now you can get started with our documentation concepts.
13
They have given so much of information.
14

15
So these are the different metrics these people have provided.
16
Task completion, step efficiency, argument correctness, tool correctness, plan quality, etc.
17
So one thing you need to remember from now on when I say testing, it means evaluating. OK.
18
So in the LLM AI world, we call them as evaluating.
19
I might use interchangeably testing, validating, but all come under the same terminology, evaluating,
20
testing, validating the systems. Right.
21
Now this task completion metric is one of the way to see how the quality of your output is.

23

24
end. OK. It's simple.
25

26

27

### RAG Metrics - Contextual Precision Metric to validate RAG Agent output quality {#rag-metrics-contextual-precision-metric-to-validat}

```
1
All right, so contextual precision.
2
What is this metric about?
3
So in the architecture, you know, when you ask a query and then it stores in the database,
4
it searches in your database and then it does not give direct answer, right?
5
It just throw the relevant documents which it thinks will be the, you know, meaningful for your query.
6
Now if you ask any questions such as refund policy, now let's go to our implementation
7
that makes you better understanding.
8
So this is our rag agent.
9
So all our policy documents for simplicity, what I did is I stored them as a text. Okay.
10
In real time, these will be retrieved from the PDF. Okay.
11
So now you can see, so all these documents, what you see here, every chunk assume that
12
it's one of the PDF document.
13
So here, if you want to know about, give me the refund policy of jewelry, right?
14
And then what happens?
15
It actually, if you ask a query, then it will check in the vector DB if there are any embeddings
16
related to the jewelry refund policy.
17
So let's say out of hundred documents, it felt four documents are, you know, which can
18
match with user query.
19
Those four documents only will be sent to the large language model and this model will
20
read those four documents and read your original query and try to answer your query from those
21
four documents with its intelligence and it gives back the response.
22
This is the rag concept.
23
And now here, I told you that it might retrieve four documents, right?
24
It usually returns some set of documents, guys.
25
Let's say in this case, four.
26
And now the contextual precision means we are checking whether nodes in your retrieval context. Okay.
27
Nodes means, let's say in this scenario, these are documents, whether documents in your retrieval
28
context that are relevant to the given input are ranked higher than irrelevant ones, meaning
29
I'll just write down a notes here to explain it.
30
It might sound a little tricky, but let me explain this quick.
31
New Python file and let's have it as rag agent. Okay.
32
So in our case, let's say five documents are retrieved from where?
33
From the vector DB to the LLM.
34
So what this metric says is about, out of five documents, let's say LLM read all the five documents.
35
So most of the time it will be in the top documents only.
36
So how it retrieves five documents?
37
So it will tell in the order, the first document which it retrieved will be the 99.9% chances
38
that your answer will be in the first document.
39
If it thinks that, you know, second document or third document could have some more answer,
40
then it will be putting in that order.
41
If it retrieved five documents, ideally 90% of your answer could be in the first document,
42
6% of your answer could be found in the second document and some final portions, you know,
43
could be in the third and fourth.
44
So that's how the order of documents this retrieval context will give.
45
So technically to say it as a good rag agent, you know, LLM has to find it in the top document itself.
46
So that means the retrieval context is so powerful because it brings in the order of
47
documents where the answer probability is more.
48
Let's say the answer is actually more found in the fourth document, 50% of the answer
49
itself is in the fourth document.
50
Then you know, the retrieval context is not proper because you are wasting LLM to read
51
until five documents to get the 50% of answer finally.
52
Why don't you provide in the first document itself?
53
